# CUDA → Slang Migration Status

Honest per-file status of the migration of the PhysX 5.6.1 GPU pipeline
(61 `.cu` files, 62 `.cuh` headers, ~95k lines of device code) to Slang with
Vulkan/D3D12 backends.

**Legend**
- ✅ **ported + verified** — Slang kernel exists, runs on Vulkan, passes the
  verification suite against a CPU transcription of the CUDA original
- 🟡 **partially ported** — core paths ported + verified, listed gaps remain
- ⬜ **not started** — still CUDA-only

## gpucommon

| CUDA source | Kernels | Status | Slang |
|---|---|---|---|
| `radixSortImpl.cu` + `RadixSort.cuh` | radixSortMultiBlockLaunch\*, radixSortMultiCalculateRanksLaunch\*, radixSortCopyHigh32Bits | ✅ | `radixSort.slang` + `radixSort64.slang` (histogram + stable reorder + 64-bit key chaining; the `WithCount`/`WithoutCount`/`BlockDesc` entry variants collapse into push-constant differences). `radixSortGather.slang` ports the remaining rank-indexed gather/copy family (radixSortCopy / radixSortCopy2 / radixSortCopyBits2 / radixSortDoubleCopy / radixSortDoubleCopyHigh32Bits) — 64-bit `uint2` gathers with aggregate-sentinel (`0xffffffff`) masking and low/high-word selection ✅. **`gpucommon` is now ported in full** (utility, MemCopyBalanced, and the whole radix-sort machinery; the single-warp in-block sort variant is subsumed by the multi-block path) |
| `reduction.cuh` (header) | warpScan/blockScan/warpReduction | ✅ | `pxgSlangScan.slang` (portable groupshared equivalents) |
| `MemCopyBalanced.cu` | MemCopyBalanced, clampMaxValue, clampMaxValues | ✅ | `memCopy.slang` + `clampValues.slang` (CopyDesc pointers → buffer byte offsets) |
| `utility.cu` | interleaveBuffers + deformable-skinning kernels (zero/areaWeighted/normalize normals, cloth Phong + softbody barycentric interpolation) | ✅ | `skinning.slang` (PxTypedBoundedData pointers → strided buffer views; float atomicAdd → portable CAS loop) |
| `updateCacheAndBound.cuh` (header) | updateBounds/getAbsPose/setTransformCache | ✅ | `updateBounds.slang` |
| other `.cuh` (atomic, copy, sparseRemove, contactReduction, gridCal, MemoryAllocator) | device helpers | 🟡 | ported on demand with their consumers (atomic.cuh AtomicAdd3 ✅ in `skinning.slang`; sparseRemove ✅ in `npPairManagement`/`npPairRemoveSwap`).  `gridCal.slang` ports gridCal.cuh in full ✅ — calcGridPos (floor cell coords), calcGridHash (pow2 `&`-wrap + z-major linear address), calcGridHashPeriodic (single-wrap periodic adjust, addressed over the non-periodic size), calcWrappedGridRangeSize (clamped [0, gridSize]), calcGridOffsetInRange (linear→3D decode), calcGridRange (bounds corners) and getParticleSpeculativeContactVolume (forward-projection midpoint + covering radius, PGS/TGS/diffuse scales) — the particle-system spatial-query foundation; grid outputs verified bit-exactly (positions generated away from cell boundaries so floor is deterministic; negative cells, both periodic wrap directions and range clamping exercised), the speculative volume to tolerance with all three scale branches exercised.  `contactReduce.slang` ports contactReduction.cuh in full ✅ — contactReduceShared and its contactReduce (adj 0, criterion = separation) / contactReduce2 (adj = −separation, criterion = projected distance to origin) wrappers: the optional duplicate cull (1e-8 squared-distance gate with the deeper-or-higher-lane tie-break), the ≤TMaxPoints early-out, the four anchor picks on the plane-projected points (extreme point, farthest from it, the two cross-axis extremes with the per-lane distance adjustment), the optional PCM extra-deepest pick, and the TClustering path (anchor separations biased by −clusterBias, nearest-anchor labeling with first-anchor tie wins, deepest-per-cluster then deepest-fill) or the plain deepest-fill.  The CUDA's warp minIndex/maxIndex (lowest-lane argmin/argmax, exact-value fmin/fmax selects) serialize to strict-comparison lowest-index loops, bit-identically; template parameters become per-case runtime flags.  The result mask is compared bit-exactly against a C++ transcription: raw-separation comparisons (dupe tie-break incl. exact-equality lane ties, all deepest-fill picks) are exact by construction, computed quantities (dupe distance gate, anchor/label near-ties) are slack-tracked and near cases skipped; all 16 flag combinations, both TMaxPoints, early-out/dupe/PCM/cluster/plain paths exercised |

## gpusolver (rigid-body PGS/TGS solver)

| CUDA source | Kernels | Status | Slang |
|---|---|---|---|
| `preIntegration.cu/.cuh` | preIntegrationLaunch, initStaticKinematics | ✅ | `preIntegration.slang` |
| `integration.cu` + `integration.cuh` | integrateCoreParallelLaunch | ✅ | `integration.slang` (PGS variant incl. full sleep/freeze/wake state machine) |
| `integrationTGS.cu` | integrateCoreParallelLaunchTGS | ✅ | `integration.slang` (integrateCoreTGS: absolute velocities, delta-transform pose accumulation) |
| `preIntegrationTGS.cu` | preIntegrationLaunchTGS | ✅ | covered by `preIntegration.slang` — the TGS entry is the identical kernel with the runtime skipGravityApplication flag, which the port already exposes as a push constant |
| `solverMultiBlock.cu` + `solverBlock.cuh` | rigid PGS pipeline | 🟡 | `solverPGS.slang` — solveBlockPartition (solveContactBlock + solve1DBlock incl. compliant/accel-spring paths, mass-splitting reference counts) ✅, concludeBlocks ✅, writebackBlocks ✅ (force buffer, joint impulse/break writeback, friction patch export, threshold stream via atomics), writeBackBodies ✅, computeAverageSolverBodyVelocity + propagateSolverBodyVelocity ✅ (multi-slab velocity averaging incl. slab-count reset), ZeroBodies ✅ (`solverZeroBodies.slang` — pre-solve reset of the motion-velocity array and solver body-velocity pool, delta-V-offset range included), dmaBackChangedElems + dmaConstraintResidual ✅ (`solverDmaBack.slang` — word-wise DMA-back of the force-change threshold elements and per-constraint residual gather from the writeback pool); remaining: markActiveSlab/solveStaticBlock/initialization glue, articulation paths (`solver.cu` artiSolveBlockPartition, solveExt*), contact-error accumulator stats ⬜ |
| `solverMultiBlockTGS.cu` + `solverBlockTGS.cuh` | rigid TGS pipeline | 🟡 | `solverTGS.slang` — solveBlockPartitionTGS (rigid path of solveBlockUnified): solveContactBlockTGS (accumulated-delta separation, compliant/accel-spring restitutionXdt path, paired anisotropic friction + torsional anchors) and solve1DBlockTGS (delta-transform Jacobian re-anchoring, ortho-axis projection, spring modes, residuals) ✅; concludeBlocksTGS ✅ (conclude1DBlockTGS bias removal; the CUDA contact conclude body is compiled out and stays a no-op), writebackBlocksTGS ✅ (force buffer, joint impulse/break writeback, friction patch export, threshold stream), writeBackBodiesTGS ✅ (accumulated deltas → motion velocities), computeAverageSolverBodyVelocityTGS + propagateAverageSolverBodyVelocityTGS ✅ (6-component mass-splitting velocity averaging incl. slab-count reset, per-substep delta-pose quaternion integration with lock flags), ZeroBodiesTGS ✅ (`solverZeroBodies.slang` — pre-solve reset of the motion-velocity array and solver body-velocity pool over the TGS body/constraint ranges), initializeSolverVelocitiesTGS ✅ (`solverInitVelocitiesTGS.slang` — seeds the body-velocity pool with each body's pre-solve velocity repacked into the 6-component/32-strided slab layout, scattered to every slab's remapped slot triple plus the accumulation-offset copy), applyTGSSubstepGravity ✅ (`solverApplyGravityTGS.slang` — per-substep gravity + external linear/angular acceleration applied to every slab copy of each body velocity, with per-body lock flags and the `sqrtInvInertia.getInverse()` angular transform); remaining: solveStaticBlockTGS, articulation paths ⬜ |
| `constraintBlockPrep.cu` + `contactConstraintBlockPrep.cuh` + `jointConstraintBlockPrep.cuh` | rigid PGS constraint prep | 🟡 | `constraintPrepPGS.slang` — contactConstraintBlockPrepareParallelLaunch (friction correlation: correlatePatches/getFrictionPatches/growPatches, and setupFinalizeSolverConstraintsBlock: contact Jacobians, resp/coeff terms, compliant contacts, tangent friction rows) ✅, jointConstraintBlockPrepareParallelLaunch (preprocessRows + orthogonalize, setUp1DConstraintBlock, intializeBlock1D: PGS solver constants, spring/bounce/drive-limit handling) ✅; articulation (`extContactConstraintPrepareLaunch`, ext 1D) paths ⬜ |
| `constraintBlockPrepTGS.cu` + `jointConstraintBlockPrepTGS.cuh` | rigid TGS constraint prep | 🟡 | `constraintPrepTGS.slang` — contactConstraintBlockPrepareParallelLaunchTGS (restitutionXdt/scaledBias TGS coefficients, torsional friction anchor, kinematic target-velocity bias) ✅, jointConstraintBlockPrepareParallelLaunchTGS (setUp1DConstraintBlockTGS + intializeBlock1DTGS: biasScale/velMultiplier/targetVel constants, maxBiasVelocity, ortho-axis header setup, solve-hint promotion) ✅; articulation paths ⬜ |
| `constraintBlockPrePrep.cu` | constraint pre-prep (partition remap, friction-patch counts) | ⬜ | produces the batch/partition layout the block prep consumes |
| `artiConstraintPrep2.cu` | articulation constraint prep | ⬜ | |
| `accumulateThresholdStream.cu` | force-threshold reporting | ✅ | `thresholdStream.slang` — sort-key extraction for the two-pass (nodeIndexA, nodeIndexB) sort (initialRanksAndBodyIndexB/A) + stream reorganize ✅, device-wide segmented force accumulation (computeAccumulateThresholdStream + outputAccumulateThresholdStream + writeoutAccumulatedForcePerObject) ✅, exceeded-force detection + compaction (computeExceededForceThresholdElementIndice + outputExceededForceThresholdElementIndice) ✅. `thresholdForceChange.slang` ports the cross-frame found/lost/persistent event diff: setThresholdElementsMask (binary-search the sorted previous set with the `ThresholdStreamElement`/`PxNodeIndex` 64-bit comparators + backward run-walk to match shapeInteraction, clearing the persistent pairs' previous/current masks), setPersistentForceElementMask (persistentMask = !previousMask), computeThresholdElementMaskIndices + outputThresholdPairsMaskIndices (two-pass device-wide exclusive prefix over the previous|current|persistent 0/1 mask) and createForceChangeThresholdElements (compact the found/lost/persistent events, lost pairs zeroing accumulatedForce) ✅. The CUDA in-kernel setPersistentForceElementMask device call — a cross-block read-after-write on the persistent region — is split into its own launch for globally-ordered, identical output. Only `bodyInputAndRanksSingleBlock/BlocksLaunch` (radix-sort setup subsumed by the generic radix path) remain outside this file's shaders |

## gpubroadphase / gpunarrowphase / gpuarticulation

| Module | Files | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| gpubroadphase | `broadphase.cu`, `aggregate.cu` | ✅ | incremental SAP; heavy consumer of the ported radix sort + scans. `broadphaseSetup.slang` ports the foundation kernels: translateAABBsLaunch (float `PxBounds3` → sortable `PxgIntegerAABB` with contact-distance inflation, monotonic float encoding, axis bit-shift and env-ID packing) ✅, markCreatedPairsLaunch (append new bounds' projections + start/end sort handles, radix padding) ✅, initializeSapBox1DLaunch (SAP-box endpoint indices from the sorted handles) ✅, initializeRadixRanks (identity ranks for the endpoint radix sort) ✅, updateHandles (reorder the previous frame's handles through the sort permutation) ✅. `broadphaseReports.slang` ports the found/lost pair report accumulation: accumulateReportsStage_1 + accumulateReportsStage_2 (classify each pair as aggregate vs actor via `VolumeData::isSingleActor` and stably compact the four found/lost × agg/actor categories through a 2-stage between-block prefix) ✅, copyReports (actor found/lost reports → mapped output, overflow flag) ✅. `broadphaseIncrementalHistogram.slang` ports the incremental comparison-count stage: computeIncrementalComparisonHistograms_Stage1 + computeIncrementalComparisonHistograms_Stage2 (per-projection overlap-comparison counts as a difference of two prefix-sum histogram entries selected from the 2×2×3 [isStartHandle][isDownPass][axis] table, deleted projections excluded, followed by a device-wide two-pass exclusive prefix producing the per-projection running total, per-block totals and grand total per axis) ✅. `broadphaseEndPtHistogram.slang` ports the upstream producer of those histograms: computeEndPtsHistogram + outputEndPtsHistogram (firstPassEndPtsHistogram/secondPassEndPtsHistogram) — per-projection start/end/all-end (end2) classification with a packed three-counter (10-bit fields) block scan, promoted device-wide to the start/end/total-end point histograms and their per-block totals, plus the compacting scatter of non-deleted handles into the ordered start/end point handle arrays; incremental (with/without removals) and full-rebuild frame handling ✅. `broadphaseIncrementalSap.slang` ports performIncrementalSAP — the sweep-and-prune core that consumes the two above stages: per-axis grid-stride work distribution over the comparison prefix sum, binary-search projection lookup, up/down-sweep swap-partner resolution through the ordered start/end handles, integer-AABB overlap-change detection (new vs old bounds inverted by sweep direction) with first-axis-of-separation dedup and group/environment-ID filtering, and atomic-compacted found/lost pair emission ✅. `broadphaseRegionHistogram.slang` ports the region prefix-sum scan pair computeRegionsHistogram/outputRegionsHistogram (the templated computeHistogram/outputHistogram device helpers, covering all four start-region and region wrappers): a plain device-wide two-pass exclusive prefix sum over the flat region-accumulator array plus its grand-total scalar ✅. `broadphaseCreateRegions.slang` ports createRegionsKernel, the region producer feeding that scan: for each box it maps the per-axis endpoint positions (boxSapBox1D) through the total-end-point histogram into a min/max region-index span, writing regionRange, the per-projection start-region and region overlap-check counts (startRegionAccum / regionAccum) ✅. `broadphaseActiveRegionHistogram.slang` ports the per-region handle-compaction pipeline: writeOutStartAndActiveRegionHistogram (stamp start/new 0-1 flags into each region slice a box touches), computeStartAndActiveRegionHistogram (in-place per-region exclusive prefix of the start+active flags, packed 16/16 and scanned together, block=512) and outputOrderedActiveRegionHistogram (scatter each box's handle into the compacted ordered start/active handle arrays) ✅. `broadphaseOverlapCheckHistogram.slang` ports the overlap-check counting + 64-bit scan trio: writeOutOverlapChecksForInsertedBoundsRegionsHistogram (per-comparison overlap-candidate count from the ordered-handle offset tables + handle/region record) and computeOverlapChecksForRegionsHistogram / outputOverlapChecksForRegionHistogram (a device-wide `PxU64` two-pass exclusive prefix sum + grand total over the counts, carried in `uint64_t` — the Vulkan device now enables shaderInt64) ✅. `broadphaseNewBoundsPairs.slang` ports generateFoundPairsForNewBoundsRegion, the final region kernel that consumes all of the above: a 64-bit grid-stride over the overlap-check total that binary-searches its (handle, region) entry, resolves the candidate other-handle from the region's compacted ordered list (start/active by isNewProjection), dedups by the max-of-min-corners region, applies the group/environment filter and the integer-AABB intersection, and atomically compacts the found pairs into the report ✅. `broadphaseMarkPairs.slang` ports the incremental delete/update marking kernels that prepare the endpoint arrays before the next sort: markRemovedPairsLaunch (OR the deleted bit `1<<2` into the previous frame's start/end sort handles and `setEmpty` the removed boxes' old integer bounds), markRemovedPairsProjectionsLaunch (invalidate the removed boxes' projections and SAP-box endpoints with `PXG_INVALID_BP_*` so the radix sort shuffles them to the tail), markUpdatedPairsLaunch (bitmap-driven — for each changed-but-not-added/removed handle refresh its projections from the new integer bounds at its SAP-box positions) and markUpdatedPairsLaunch2 (the same refresh driven by an explicit updated-handle array) — pure per-thread grid-stride scatter with per-axis arrays packed into flat buffers ✅. `broadphaseClearNewFlag.slang` ports the two remaining glue kernels: clearNewFlagLaunch (after the first frame that inserted a batch of newly created boxes, rewrite the current frame's — `boxHandles[0]` — start/end sort handles for those boxes with the "new" flag cleared, `createHandle(h, isStart, false)`, so they are no longer treated as freshly inserted) and bpSignalComplete (single-thread store of 1 into the completion-signal word the CPU polls) ✅. **`broadphase.cu` is now ported in full** (all 31 kernels — the two start-region histogram wrappers reuse the generic region scan). The aggregate broadphase (`aggregate.cu`) is now under way: `broadphaseAggregateBounds.slang` ports the aggregate bounds/bitmap refresh pair — markAggregateBoundsUpdatedBitmap (per valid aggregate, atomic-OR the aggregate's own AABB-manager handle bit into the changed bitmap and atomic-AND-clear each member shape's bit so members are not independently re-broadphased) and updateAggregateBounds (warp/group reduction of the union of each aggregate's contact-distance-inflated member bounds, written back at the aggregate's own bound slot). The `PxgAggregate` device-pointer members (`updateBoundIndices`) are flattened into a shared pool buffer indexed by a per-aggregate base offset, and the CUDA warp-per-aggregate layout is mapped to one 32-thread group per aggregate ✅. `broadphaseAggregateDirty.slang` ports the dirty-aggregate double-buffering pair — updateDirtyAggregate (scatter each dirty aggregate's new member-bound indices into its bound storage, then merge the staging aggregate into the persistent one: a full struct copy — the CUDA `warpCopy(sizeof(PxgAggregate))` — when the persistent aggregate is brand new, else a selective field update that preserves `prevSize` as the previous frame's size) and clearDirtyAggregates (clear the new flag + fix `prevSize` at the end of the step). `PxgAggregate` is modelled as a flat 8-uint record, with a uniform group barrier guarding the read of `isNew` before the full copy overwrites it ✅. `broadphaseAggregatePairsAdd.slang` ports the aggregate-pair add stages — addAggPairsStage1 (allocate a slot per found pair from the free-buffer list: reuse a freed hole below the high-water mark, counting down the free list, else append past `maxIndex`, skipping slots past the cap, and write the pair `isNew`/`isDead`) and addAggPairsStage2 (a single thread finalizes the free-list counts — consume freed indices or grow the high-water mark — and raises the aggregate-pair and found-pair overflow flags, the overflow count captured pre-clamp) ✅. `broadphaseAggregateFinalize.slang` ports three finalization kernels — aggCopyReports (clamp the found/lost aggregate pair reports to the cap, copy them to their mapped output buffers and raise the overflow flags — found via `|=`, lost via `=`), removeAggPairsStage3 (return the removed pair slots to the free list) and markAddedAndDeletedAggregatedBounds (setEmpty the integer bounds of added/deleted aggregated shapes with a 2-row group — old bounds for both, new bounds only for deleted) ✅. `broadphaseAggregatePairsRemove.slang` ports the agg-pair removal compaction — removeAggPairsStage1 (per-block count of the pairs flagged for removal in a remove bitmap) and removeAggPairsStage2 (between-block exclusive prefix + a stable compaction that appends each removed pair's index to the free list in ascending index order, marks the pair dead and records the total removed). The CUDA 32-block ballot/`__shfl` decomposition is replaced by the verified broadphaseReports two-pass groupshared-scan structure (32 blocks of 256 threads); since the result is a stable compaction keyed on the pair index it is grid-geometry-independent and bit-identical ✅. `broadphaseAggregateBoxPruning.slang` ports the aggregate collision SAP sweep primitive — bipartiteBoxPruning (two boxPruningProjection passes) plus its helpers (getIthBit / getIndex / countComparisons / bSearchFirstProjection / binarySearch1 / integer-AABB intersects): given two box lists and their sorted X-projection SAP structure, it emits every cross-list pair whose new integer bounds intersect but whose old bounds do not (group-filtered, deduplicated across the two projection directions). The per-warp `__ballot_sync`/`__popc`/`__shfl`/warpScan machinery is replaced by one 32-thread group per sweep with groupshared 32-lane scans and `InterlockedAdd` pair emission; the sweep is the shared inner loop of doSelfCollision and doAggPairCollisions ✅. `broadphaseAggPairCollision.slang` ports doAggPairCollisions, which wraps that sweep: per aggregate pair (one 32-thread group) it skips dead pairs, marks the pair removed if either of its bounds was deleted this frame, otherwise decides whether it should still be removed (its aggregate-level new bounds no longer intersect), runs the bipartite sweep between the two sides' member boxes to emit found pairs (new intersect / not old) and — for non-new pairs — lost pairs on the previous frame's structure (old intersect / not new), then clears the new flag. Each side is an aggregate (member SAP structure read from shared pools indexed per aggregate and per frame) or a single actor (a synthesized 1-box structure built into groupshared) ✅. `broadphaseAggSelfCollision.slang` ports doSelfCollision: for each valid, self-collision-enabled aggregate (one 32-thread group) it counts, per member box, how many other members' start projections fall strictly within its X span (from sapBox1D + startMasks, the `2<<` strictly-after exclusion), prefix-sums those into per-box offsets and a grand total, then generates found pairs (members that now overlap but did not last frame) and — for non-new aggregates — lost pairs on the previous frame's structure, and records the total as the next frame's previous-comparison count ✅. `broadphaseAggSortProjections.slang` ports sortAndUpdateAggregateProjections, the producer that the collision kernels consume: for each valid aggregate (one 32-thread group) it builds the per-member min/max X projections and start/end handles from updateBoundIndices + newIntegerBounds, sorts the projections by value, and computes the per-box SAP-box positions (sapBox1D), the start-projection bitmask and the local bound-index list; the CUDA warp radix sort is replaced by an output-identical stable rank sort (radix sort is stable, so ties break by original index, matching the rank's tie-break) ✅. **`gpubroadphase` is now ported in full — `broadphase.cu` (31 kernels) and `aggregate.cu` (14 kernels).** |
| gpunarrowphase | 25 files (GJK/EPA, convex/mesh/HF, midphase, contact management) | 🟡 | largest module; `updateBounds.slang` established the shape/geometry data-structure mapping (hull blob offsets) it needs. `npPairManagement.slang` opens the module with the sparse-remove contact-manager compaction core (`pairManagement.cu` + `SparseRemove.cuh`): initializeKeepDropBuffer + markKeepDropBuff (build the keep/drop mark buffer — 1 for removed elements in the kept region, cleared for kept elements pulled from the tail), processKeepDropBuff + accumulateKeepDrop (the device-wide two-pass exclusive prefix sum over the marks producing the run-sum the swap stage consumes) and initializeManifolds (shared-memory transpose broadcast of one manifold structure into N copies) ✅. `npPairRemoveSwap.slang` completes the removal with the Stage5 data swap — removeContactManagers_Stage5 (16 threads/swap, single manifold) and removeContactManagers_Stage5_CvxTri (32 threads/swap, multi-manifold): getNbSwapsRequired = runSum[N-K], getSwapIndices via binarySearch, moving each kept tail element into a removed hole in the kept region (swaps are provably disjoint, so one thread-group per swap is output-identical) ✅. `npSphereCollision.slang` ports the single-contact sphere-primitive collision routines from `cudaSphere.cu`/`sphereCollision.cuh` — spheresphere, sphereplane, spherecapsule and spherebox (point + penetration + world normal, incl. the box inside-penetration/face branches and the point-segment distance for the capsule; quaternion/transform math reuses the shared pxgSlangCommon helpers) ✅. `npCapsuleCollision.slang` adds the two-contact capsule routines planeCapsule and capsuleCapsule (with distanceSegmentSegmentSquared + pcmDistancePointSegmentTValue22): the general closest-features contact, the near-parallel endpoint-projection path yielding up to two contacts, and the inline degenerate case ✅. `npMaterialCombine.slang` ports PxsCombineMaterials (the material-combining core of insertIntoPatchStream): combined friction/restitution/damping/flags with the per-property combine modes, the compliant (negative-restitution) and acceleration-spring special cases, and the friction-disable path ✅. `npContactPatch.slang` ports the contact-stream allocation glue: setContactPointAndForcePointers (atomic reservation of the per-pair contact + force/indice byte ranges with overflow detection) and registerContactPatch (the PxsContactManagerOutput status-flag bit-packing — allflagsStart/nbPatches/statusFlag/prevPatches — plus the touch/patch-change flags and the atomic patch reservation) ✅. `npInsertPatch.slang` ports insertIntoPatchStream — combines the two shapes' materials and writes the 64-byte PxContactPatch header (mMassModification, normal, the combined friction/restitution/damping scalars, and the packed startContactIndex/nbContacts/materialFlags/internalFlags/materialIndex0/1 words) at the registered patch offset ✅. **The contact-output machinery (allocation → patch registration → material combine → patch write) is now ported in full**. `npCompressContacts.slang` ports two of the three `compressOutputContacts.cu` kernels — compressContactStage1 (per-block count of the pairs that produced ≥1 contact, the histogram the Stage2 compaction consumes) and updateFrictionPatches (rescale each pair's contact-patch byte offset into the friction-patch stream, `×sizeof(PxFrictionPatch)/sizeof(PxContactPatch)`) ✅. `npCompressContacts2.slang` completes the trio with compressContactStage2 — the cross-block compaction of every pair with contacts into the dense PxGpuContactPair list: the Stage1 per-block counts are exclusive-prefix-summed to each block's start offset (and the grand total), then the fixed BLOCK/NUM_BLOCKS partition is re-walked and each surviving pair written to slot `blockStart + within-block running survivor count`, with the pointer→byte-offset conversions (contactPatches/points/forces, the friction-patch rescale) and remap/actor-table lookups; the CUDA warp-ballot within-block prefix is replaced by an output-identical block-wide groupshared scan (same stable work order), verified bit-exactly against a C++ transcription of the whole compaction incl. the numPairs total ✅. **The `compressOutputContacts.cu` contact-compression trio is now ported in full.** `npTriangle.slang` opens the mesh/heightfield triangle-collision geometry with the two self-contained primitives shared across those paths (`sphereTriangle.cuh`/`triangle.cuh`): distancePointTriangleSquared (the Ericson closest-point-on-triangle test — squared distance, closest point, the Voronoi-region mask V0/V1/V2/E01/E02/E12/face, and the edge-convexity-gated generateContact/faceContact flags) and intersectLineTri (the one-sided line-vs-triangle test), verified against C++ transcriptions (closest point/distance to tolerance, the discrete region mask + flags exact) with the Voronoi-region decision boundaries margin-skipped and all 7 regions exercised ✅. `npSegTriangle.slang` adds distanceSegmentTriangleSquared (capsuleTriangle.cuh) — the squared distance between a segment and a triangle with the segment parameter + triangle The heightfield SPECIALITY is the edge-concavity switch — for an eE01/eE12/eE02 closest feature the ADJACENT triangle is re-extracted and generateContact is re-gated by whether the current triangle's opposite vertex sits BELOW the adjacent face plane (`triNormalAdj . (vk - v0Adj) < 0`, concave edge accepted / convex edge rejected), with a BOUNDARY guard added as a DELIBERATE resolution of an upstream unguarded getTriangle(0xFFFFFFFF) read (a grid-edge contact has no occluding neighbour, so it is kept). Its 18-mutation battery targets the driver *glue* (init, loop control, dispatch, backup restore, output classification): 11 caught directly; the other 7 are covered by composition — one equivalent (the inert comp-3 init lane), one benign loop-tail boundary, and five arithmetic-boundary/sub-piece-internal mutants each already pinned by the corresponding standalone sub-piece test in the same ctest suite (the `lb>lowerBound`, `cm>=upperBound`, permute-`L<12` and `lowerBound>minSep` boundaries live in tranches 125/126/127).  The warm-start cache path (128) is retained but not exercised by the cold-start test.  Adversarial review surfaced one deliberate hardening of a CUDA undefined-behaviour: the CUDA declares `bool degenerate;` uninitialised (gjk.cuh:268) and reads it into `output.degenerate` on the first-iteration DISTANT break before it is ever assigned; since PhysX documents `degenerate` as meaningful only for a CLOSE result, the port defines it as `false` there — the sole intentional non-bit-identical point, unobservable to any correct caller.  `npEpaStart4.slang` opens the EPA (Expanding Polytope Algorithm) module with epaStart4 (epa.cuh :254-278, tranche 130) — when GJK ends on a full tetrahedron simplex (size 4), EPA seeds its polytope directly from it: this unpacks the 4 cached vertices + index words into the EpaScratch vertex/index arrays and normalises each of the 4 face normals the GJK simplex already carries (n·(1/√dot(n,n)), distance = n.w·(1/√dot(n,n))) into the 4 seed triangles, emitting the fixed tetrahedron topology (sStartVertices4 / sStartAdjacencies4).  m = 1/sqrt(dot) is a correctly-rounded OpFDiv-over-OpSqrt so integer simplex data stays bit-exact; 12/13 mutations caught (the survivor is the `tI<16` unpack guard, whose removal only corrupts memory in real CUDA — out of range of the bounds-checked per-lane arrays here, so unobservable) ✅.  `npEpaStart123.slang` ports epaStart123 (epa.cuh :330-385, tranche 131) — the seed path when GJK ends on a lower simplex (size 1/2/3): it builds a double-sided triangle, filling missing vertices with newPoint support points (size<2 → newPoint(dir=(1,0,0)); size<3 → newPoint(dir = axis×(v1−v0))), forming the face normal from the better-conditioned edge pair (|e01|≤|e02| ? e01×e12 : e02×e12).getNormalized() with distance = n·v0, and emitting the two-triangle sStartVertices3/sStartAdjacency3 topology (lane 1 negated).  Reuses the ported newPoint/supportIndex argmax; bit-exact for integer clouds; 12/15 mutations caught + 3 proven equivalent — because e02 = e01+e12 and cross(e12,e12)=0 exactly, cross(e01,e12) = cross(e02,e12) = cross(e01,e02) bit-for-bit, so the |e01|≤|e02| edge-pair conditioning choice is unobservable (the getNormalized zero-guard is pinned by a collinear-vertex fixture) ✅.  `npEpa.slang` assembles the **full epa() driver** (epa.cuh :388-526, tranche 132) — the Expanding Polytope Algorithm that runs on GJK's overlap simplex to find the minimum translational distance.  It wires the seed functions epaStart4/epaStart123 (130/131) to the intricate replaceTriangle topology update (epa.cuh :64-251 — a ballot flood-fill of the triangles silhouetted by each new vertex, a single-thread linked-list flatten of the silhouette loop, and a cone of replacement triangles with popcount/ballot adjacency) and the main loop (pick the closest live triangle, add its support vertex, replaceTriangle, until the support distance meets the face distance within releps), finishing with the barycentric witness points.  One thread runs the whole warp-serialised polytope (up to EPA_MAX_FACES=32 triangles / EPA_MAX_VERTS=18 vertices).  Tested end-to-end by chaining a bit-exact CPU transcription of the GJK driver (129) to produce ~800 genuine overlap simplices from integer hull pairs, then feeding each to the EPA shader — the result enum, bounds, degenerate flag, direction and both witness points match a line-for-line EPA CPU transcription bit-for-bit (zero divergence across all runs) ✅.  Its 17-mutation battery on the driver glue (main-loop convergence/bounds, threatened/dead/cone topology, support direction, result classification) caught 12 directly, including the replaceTriangle internals (deadB construction, silhouette-edge test, nbLoop consistency, cone conditioning); the 5 survivors are all exact-float-equality / rare-degeneracy boundaries (abs(Δ)==releps, e==upperBound, normal·apex==releps, the fail tolerance, lowerBound==0 touching) that random integer hulls do not hit — validated instead by the ~800-run bit-exact end-to-end comparison.  `npGjkWarm.slang` extends the driver to the full cold+warm gjk() (gjk.cuh :243-418, tranche 133): with a non-empty input GjkCachedData it sets cacheMask=(1<<size)−1, seeds the simplex from the cache (closestPointV=splatV(0,simplex)), and each iteration runs the warm-start candidate fold (tranche 128 — ballot of the cached verts closer than the current feature, folding the lowest back in) before falling back to fresh support vertices; a geometric degeneracy while warm-started resets cacheMask and restarts cold (CUDA's `goto restart`, modelled as an outer restart loop).  Tested end-to-end by running a bit-exact cold GJK to produce a genuine cache, then feeding it back warm — the shader matches a line-for-line CPU transcription bit-for-bit across both the ~900 cold and ~600 warm calls (incl. warm overlaps that seed EPA) ✅.  14-mutation battery on the warm-specific additions: 11 caught (cacheMask init/guards, the candidate-fold ballot/gather/mask, lowestSetBit/cacheMask-clear/splatV index, and the inverted fold branch which hangs → caught); the 3 survivors are all in the rarely-triggered degenerate-while-warm-started `goto restart` path (a coverage gap — well-conditioned integer hulls converge normally and seldom hit a mid-loop degeneracy), validated instead by the bit-exact ~1500-run comparison and the adversarial review, which confirmed the restart structure and every warm-start item faithful.  `npCvxCvxEE.slang` ports the convex-convex edge-edge contact generator generateEE() from `cudaGJKEPA.cu` (~L1144, tranche 134) — distinct from the capsule/convex-triangle generateEE (which gate on squared distance vs a squared radius): the reference edge (a,b) is crossed with the colliding edge (p,q) about the contact normal (n=ab×normal, the signP·signQ straddle test, the pq/plane intersection localPointA, the 2D perNormal=normal×pq segment/segment solve for tValue clipped to the *expanded* span [−0.005, 1.005]), and a hit within the *signed* distance inflatedRadius emits localPointB=localPointA−v, localPointA=aToB.transformInv(localPointA) and (normal, signedDist); tested bit-exact over 16k random crossing/miss fixtures (ill-conditioned small-|denom| cases margin-skipped since localPointA/v/localPointB scale like 1/denom).  22-mutation battery: 16 caught; the 6 survivors are all equivalent mutants — n=cross(normal,ab) and perNormal=cross(pq,normal) negate both numerator and denominator of a quotient so the result is bit-identical (m6 proven 0/2M diff, m7 0/2M except a degenerate ±inf divide that the range-check rejects), dot(n,b)↔dot(n,a) differ only by the rounding residual of the numerically-perpendicular n·ab (sub-tolerance in the guarded regime), and `>`↔`>=` on temp==0 / tValue==maxT / inflatedRadius==signedDist are measure-zero exact-boundary flips covered by the marginal-skip guards; the adversarial review found no divergence.  `npCvxRayPoly.slang` ports intersectRayPolyhedron() from `cudaGJKEPA.cu` (~L1025, tranche 135) — the ray/convex-polyhedron slab clip that finds the [tEnter, tExit] parameter interval the ray a+t·dir spends inside the polyhedron (per face: a plane nearly parallel to the ray with the base point outside separates → miss; otherwise the crossing tTemp=−distToPlane/denom raises tEnter when entering (denom<0) or lowers tExit when exiting (denom>0); tEnter>tExit ⇒ miss).  The CUDA processes 32 faces per warp and all-reduces max(tEnter)/min(tExit)/OR(separation); the port runs one thread per problem and serialises the warp faithfully — within each block of 32 faces every face's con0/con1 compare against the *block-entry* (shared) tEnter/tExit and the block folds max/min from that baseline (exactly the parallel identity), never mid-block, so the serial order is irrelevant (max/min associative-commutative).  Tested bit-exact over 14k rotated-box + sphere-tangent-hull fixtures (the hulls exercise the two-block path), including hand-built axis-aligned `dir.x==0` cases that deterministically drive the |denom|<eps parallel branch (unreachable by random rays).  19-mutation battery: 17 caught (incl. the parallel-branch separation flip, once the parallel fixtures were added); the 2 survivors are exact-`>`↔`>=` boundary flips (distToPlane==0 exactly on a parallel plane, tEnter==tExit grazing) — measure-zero and covered by the marginal-skip guards; adversarial review confirmed the serialisation equivalence with no divergence.  `npCvxFaceContacts.slang` ports generatedFaceContacts() from `cudaGJKEPA.cu` (~L1099, tranche 136) — the convex-face contact emitter that composes tranche 135: for each of the two GJK witness verts vA[0], vA[1] it casts a vertex-space ray (shape2Vertex(vA_i), shape2Vertex(gjkDir)) through the polyhedron via the inlined intersectRayPolyhedron and, when the ray enters within contactDistance, emits a contact (va=aToB.transformInv(vA_i), vb=vA_i−dir·tEnter with dir=−gjkDir, normalPen=(dir, tEnter)) into slots 0..1 (slot = prior count).  Tested bit-exact over 14k box/tilted-hull fixtures with identity and mild non-identity scale/rot (exercising shape2Vertex), the witness verts positioned via the vertex2Shape inverse so the vertex-space rays hit, contactDistance swept for a 0/1/2-contact mix.  13-mutation battery: all 13 caught (dir sign, the shape2Vertex `/scale` and its −dir argument, both inflatedRadius≥tEnter gates, the vb sign for each vert, transformInv's v−p, the slot-selection `==0u`, p0↔vA and the second-vert vA1 use, and the inlined intersect divide confirming the composed clip is exercised).  Full ctest 409/409; adversarial review found no divergence.  `npCvxReduceManifold.slang` ports the schlock::reduce() manifold contact reduction from `cudaGJKEPA.cu` (active #else variant ~L415, the reduce&lt;false,false,4&gt; instantiation used by addManifoldPoint, tranche 137) — given nbContacts&gt;4 candidate contacts (one per warp lane) it selects the four extreme points: i0=deepest (min separation), i1=furthest from i0 (max squared distance), f=argmax and g=argmin of d=[normal×(p_i1−p_i0)]·(p−p_i0), with g re-picked as the second argmax when d[f]·d[g] share a sign (both on one side).  minIndex/maxIndex are the butterfly argmin/argmax over a lane mask returning the LOWEST lane holding the extreme value; the port runs one thread per problem and serialises them over per-lane arrays.  Callers only invoke reduce with popc&gt;4 so every allMask&~mask stays non-empty (the port mirrors that contract), and the result is always a 4-bit lane mask.  Tested bit-exact over 14k fixtures (nbContacts 5..32, per-lane points/separations/normals) comparing the selected 4-bit mask exactly, with argmin/argmax near-tie groups and the near-zero d[f]·d[g] sign gate margin-skipped (measure-zero GPU-vs-CPU flips), plus dedicated exact-duplicate-minimum-separation fixtures (bit-identical ties are deterministic, not skipped) that exercise the lowest-lane tie-break.  15-mutation battery: 13 caught (incl. the firstbitlow→firstbithigh tie-break flip once the exact-tie fixtures were added, and a build-fail from referencing i1 early); the 2 survivors are equivalent mutants — d's base point p_i0→p_i1 shifts d by dir·(p_i1−p_i0), a scalar triple product with a repeated vector = 0 exactly (empirically max 1.1e-5 in float, sub-tolerance), and searching allMask vs allMask&~{i0} for the furthest point is identical since p_i0 has squared-distance 0 to itself and is never the argmax (empirically never selected).  (The reduce&lt;TClustering=true&gt; post-cull path is dead code — never instantiated anywhere in the tree; every call site is reduce&lt;false,false,4&gt; and the sole &lt;false,true,…&gt; use in convexTriangle.cuh is commented out, with TKeepAnotherDeepestForPCM unused in the active variant body — so tranche 137 covers the entire reachable behaviour of schlock::reduce.)  Full ctest 411/411; adversarial review found no divergence.  `npCvxWitnessPoly.slang` ports getPolygonIndexFromLocalWitness() from `cudaGJKEPA.cu` (~L611, tranche 138) — given a witness point + direction in shape space and the convex polygon planes (vertex space), it picks the best-matching polygon: transform the witness to vertex space (shape2Vertex), per-polygon signed plane distance clamped to +inf when behind by more than the tolerance (=clamp(radius·0.01, tolLen·1e-2, tolLen·5e-2)); if none is in front pick the deepest-behind (index 0 in the reachable case), else among the polygons within tolerance of the closest pick the one whose vertex-space normalized plane normal has the smallest projection onto the witness normal.  The CUDA strides polygons across 32 warp lanes and combines with warpReduction (min/max, winner = lowest lane holding the extreme via `lowestSetIndex(ballot(best==input))`); the port replicates the 32-lane striding + lowest-lane tie-break faithfully (a plain linear scan would return a different index when the extreme is achieved in more than one lane).  Tested bit-exact over 14k fixtures (sphere-tangent hulls of 4..48 faces exercising the two-lane-block path, witness placed via the vertex2Shape inverse near the surface or deep inside, ¼ with mild non-identity scale/rot exercising shape2Vertex/getNormalized) comparing the selected index exactly, with the pd≥−tolerance gate, the within-tolerance gate, and the projection-argmin winner margin-skipped when within 1.5e-4 of a boundary (measure-zero flips; the lane tie-break is then irrelevant since the extremum is unique).  15-mutation battery: 14 caught; the 1 survivor is an equivalent mutant — the pass-1 max-distance index is only read in the all-behind branch (where the lowest-lane reduce winner is lane 0, whose first polygon is index 0, so the result is 0 regardless of the `>`↔`<` flip) and as the pass-2 initial index (always overwritten by a qualifying polygon before it can surface, since the closest plane always satisfies the tolerance gate).  Full ctest 413/413; adversarial review found no divergence.  `npCvxLocalNormal.slang` ports calculateLocalNormal() from `cudaGJKEPA.cu` (~L510, tranche 139) — the average contact normal of a persistent-contact manifold (≤PXG_MAX_PCM_CONTACTS=4 contacts): it sums the first numContacts normals and returns them normalized.  The CUDA sums with a width-4 warp tree reduction (shuffle tI+1 then tI+2), which fixes the summation to the *pairwise* (n0+n1)+(n2+n3) association — float addition is non-associative, so the port reproduces exactly that grouping (out-of-range slots contributing zero) rather than a left-fold.  Tested bit-exact over 12k fixtures (numContacts 1..4, clustered + fully-random normals) comparing the normalized result to tolerance, near-degenerate sums (tiny magnitude) margin-skipped.  Full ctest 415/415; 8-mutation battery: 7 caught; the 1 survivor is an equivalent mutant (the getNormalized m==0 else-branch returns v instead of 0, but dot(v,v)==0 ⟺ v is exactly the zero vector, so 0 and v are identical); adversarial review found no divergence.  `npCvxAddManifold.slang` ports addManifoldPoint() from `cudaGJKEPA.cu` (~L553, tranche 140) — inserts a new contact into a persistent manifold (≤4 points): each existing point flags a match if its A or B endpoint is within replaceBreakingThreshold of the new one; if any match, or the manifold is not yet full, the new point replaces the lowest matching slot (or appends, numContacts++); otherwise (full, matchless) it runs the schlock::reduce (tranche 137) 4-extreme selection over the 5 candidates — the 4 existing points in lanes 0..3 plus the new one in lane 4, reduced over their B endpoints — and compacts the chosen four back into the manifold via writeContacts (warpScanExclusive).  The port serialises the 5-lane warp (reduce + compaction) one thread per problem; the selected manifold slot values are bit-exact copies of the inputs (the reduce only selects), so only the selection can differ. Full ctest 417/417; 15-mutation battery: 14 caught (incl. a build-fail from referencing i1 early); the 1 survivor is the same equivalent mutant as the tranche-137 reduce — d's base cB[i0]→cB[i1] shifts d by dir·(cB[i1]−cB[i0]), a scalar triple product with a repeated vector = 0 exactly (≤1e-5 in float, sub-tolerance); adversarial review found no divergence.  `npCvxBlob.slang` opens the convex-hull blob-layout foundation (tranche 141) for the remaining convex-convex drivers: the bit-field primitives from `cutil_math.h` (u32High=v>>16, u32Low=v&0xffff, u16High=(u16)>>8, u16Low=v&0xff, merge16/merge8) and the packed-descriptor accessors from `cudaNpCommon.h` (getVRef8/getNbEdges=u32High, getNbVerts=u16High(u32Low), getMinIndex/getNbPolygons/getNbAdjVerts=u16Low(u32Low), the valency count/offset pack/unpack), plus the blob sub-array byte offsets (polygon planes = 32+16·nbV, poly descriptors = +16·nbP, vertex-index data = 32+19·nbV+20·nbP+6·nbE from the sizeof(uint4)+sizeof(float4)+float4·nbV +(float4+PxU32)·nbP +(PxU16+PxU8)·2·nbE +PxU8·3·nbV layout). Full ctest 419/419; 14-mutation battery: all 14 caught (pure-integer, no equivalents); adversarial review found no divergence.  `npCvxBlobRead.slang` builds on it (tranche 142) — reads real convex-hull data from a raw uint blob buffer via the T141 offsets: vertex float4 (byte 32+16·i), polygon plane float4 (planesOff+16·k), poly descriptor u32 (polyDescOff+4·k) and the byte-addressed polygon-vertex-index table u8 (vertexDataOff+idx = (buf[off/4]>>(8·(off&3)))&0xff), plus header-word field extraction — using asfloat on RWStructuredBuffer&lt;uint&gt; (the bodyUpdate/directApi pattern).
